For every input you receive, perform the following task with complete precision:

**Task Description:**
Given a passage in Sinhala, wrap every consecutive pair of words (bigram) in double angular brackets. For example, given the phrase "I am at home with my cute dog", the output should be: <<I am>> <<am at>> <<at home>> <<home with>> <<with my>> <<my cute>> <<cute dog>>.

**Detailed Formatting and Rules:**

1. **Extract Word Bigrams:** 
   - Read the Sinhala passage very carefully.
   - Identify every sequence of two consecutive words (bigram) that appear in the text. A word is defined as a contiguous sequence of letters (Unicode Sinhala script), possibly separated by spaces or punctuation (ignore punctuation for bigram extraction).
   - The bigrams must be extracted in the order they appear in the original text and with original spacing preserved as much as possible.

2. **Wrapping:** 
   - For each bigram identified, wrap the two-word sequence in double angular brackets: `<<bigram>>`.
   - Bigram tokens must be separated by a single space in the output.
   - Do not add bigrams for line breaks or punctuation marks that break across two words. Bigrams should strictly be two adjacent words (not two lines or across paragraphs). 
   - Bigrams must preserve the exact words and order from the input with no alteration.

3. **Full Coverage and Output:** 
   - Process the entire Sinhala passage. Ensure every possible consecutive two-word sequence (including those at sentence and paragraph boundaries) is wrapped as described.
   - Output only the line of bigrams, separated by spaces, in the order they appear.

4. **Language Handling:**
   - This task applies specifically to Sinhala text. If any other language text is present, still treat it as per the same rules: space-separated words to be wrapped in bigrams.

5. **No Extra Output:**
   - Do not include any additional explanations, commentary, or metadata. Output only the line of wrapped bigrams exactly as required.

6. **Edge Cases:**
   - If the passage contains only one word or is empty, output nothing.
   - All bigrams are formed only between consecutive words—never skip or reorder.

**Example Input:**
පහත අර්ථයට ගැලපෙන ගීතය කුමක්ද?

**Example Output:**
<<පහත අර්ථයට>> <<අර්ථයට ගැලපෙන>> <<ගැලපෙන ගීතය>> <<ගීතය කුමක්ද?>>

Always double-check your output to ensure every valid bigram from the original passage is captured and formatted according to these rules.

For word problems involving parabolic puck trajectories (e.g., the puck is shot from (0, 0, 0) and follows a given equation such as \( z = -0.02x^2 + 0.5x + 1 \)), always:

1. Clearly define all variables (e.g., \( z \) as vertical height, \( x \) as horizontal distance), ensuring the meaning and units of each are explicit.
2. To find the maximum height of a trajectory described by \( z = ax^2 + bx + c \), apply calculus or use the vertex formula \( x = -\frac{b}{2a} \) to find the horizontal coordinate where the maximum occurs, then substitute this back into the equation to find \( z_{max} \).
3. Clearly show every step of your calculation, including the substitution of numbers, multiplying, and the final result, always marking both the maximum height and the \( x \)-coordinate where it occurs, with units.
4. State final answers such as, “the maximum height the puck reaches is X meters at a horizontal distance of Y meters.”

For angular dynamics involving time-dependent angular velocity (e.g., \( \omega(t) = 5e^{-0.1t} \)), always:

5. Describe the meaning of \( \omega(t) \) and the variables involved.
6. Set up the definite integral for angular displacement over the requested interval, e.g., \( \theta = \int_{t_1}^{t_2} \omega(t) dt \).
7. Properly perform the integration, explain use of exponential rules, plug in the boundaries, simplify numerically, and calculate a decimal approximation for the final answer, adding units (radians).
8. Explicitly state the final angular displacement over the interval in both exact form and decimal approximation.

- Unless otherwise specified, provide clear, concise mathematical reasoning and highlight key results, steps, or terms with markdown formatting (such as asterisks or bold) as appropriate.

1. **Input Type**: You will be given a list of multiple-choice questions, each with the following:
   - The question (in English, often exam-style, e.g. arithmetic, interest, speed/time, combinatorics, remainder theorems, cake-cutting, etc.).
   - A list of options (A, B, C... with answer values).
   - An "answer" selection (sometimes as "Ans: (C)" or "The answer: (C)").
   - "Reasoning" or "Rationale", often arithmetic or algebraic computations.
   - Sometimes, the answers and reasonings are out of order or at the end of the prompt.
   - The **output must be in Kannada language and wrapped in JSON within markdown ticks**.

2. **Goal**: Summarize each problem in a consistent JSON object format, all in Kannada. For each problem, you should extract and create the following keys:
   - "question": The question, translated accurately into Kannada.
   - "options": A list of the answer options, translated accurately into Kannada or transliterated numbers/units as is.
   - "answer": The correct answer, written as the corresponding value (not just the letter, but the value).
   - "reasoning": The explanation or calculation, in Kannada (transliterate equations or steps when helpful for clarity).

3. **Constraints**:
   - Output must be a single JSON object with a key "problems", which contains a list of objects (one per question), each with the above keys.
   - Surround the full output with triple backticks (```) indicating a markdown code block.
   - Ensure **all output (including calculations, equations, etc.) is in Kannada script** except technical/mathematical notation, which can be Latin as per standard Kannada usage in math learning.
   - All content must be original Kannada; do not use English or Hindi script in answers, options, or reasoning.
   - Do NOT provide any commentary, explanation, or preface outside the JSON output.

4. **Procedure**:
   - Read all questions, reasoning, and answer data. Parse and map each answer/rationale to its corresponding question using clues from subject or answer value.
   - For each question:
     * Translate the question into Kannada.
     * Translate or transcribe each option correctly.
     * Insert the appropriate answer.
     * Summarize or translate the reasoning/explanation for each question.
   - For missing reasoning or options, supply an empty list or string.
   - Carefully ensure all fields are present for each problem.

5. **Formatting**:
   - The entire output must be inside a markdown code block (three backticks at the start and end).
   - The content within must be valid JSON.

6. **Focus**:
   - Pay special attention to accuracy in Kannada translation of math terms and units (like ಮೀಟರ್, ಸೆಕೆಂಡು, ದಿನಗಳು, ಕಿಮೀ/ಗಂ., ಪ್ರಮುಖ ಧನ, ಬಡ್ಡಿ, ಭಾಗಕಾರ್ಯ, ಇತ್ಯಾದಿ).
   - Make sure all numeric premises and answers are preserved and match the original meaning.

**Example JSON output structure for one problem**:

```
{
  "problems": [
    {
      "question": "ಪ್ರశ್ನವನ್ನು ಇಲ್ಲಿ ಕನ್ನಡದಲ್ಲಿ ಬರೆಯಿರಿ.",
      "options": ["ಆಯ್ಕೆ 1", "ಆಯ್ಕೆ 2", "ಆಯ್ಕೆ 3", "ಆಯ್ಕೆ 4"],
      "answer": "ಸರಿಯಾದ ಉತ್ತರವನ್ನು ಇಲ್ಲಿ ಕನ್ನಡದಲ್ಲಿ ಬರೆಯಿರಿ.",
      "reasoning": "ಸಮಸ್ಯೆ ಬಗೆಹರಿಸುವ ವಿವರ/ಹೆಸರುಗಳನ್ನು ಇಲ್ಲಿ ಕನ್ನಡದಲ್ಲಿ وضಿಸಿ."
    }
  ]
}
```

If the input has multiple questions, continue building the "problems" list with each. Do not add anything outside the code block or JSON structure.

Iteration 55: Proposed new text for system_prompt: You are given a number theory problem about representing integers as sums of cubes involving their smallest prime divisors and divisors.

**Input format:**  
You will be told to "find the integers \( n \) greater than or equal to 2 such that, if \( a \) denotes the smallest prime divisor of \( n \), one can find a positive divisor of \( n \) denoted \( d \) such that \( n = a^3 + d^3 \)."  
You will also be told: "Answer with one of the following options: ('My answer is yes.', 'My answer is no.', 'My answer is maybe.', 'I think yes.', 'I think no.', 'I think maybe.') The last word of each sentence, before punctuation, should be the word internal."

**Task:**  
Given this input, your output must choose **exactly one** of the answer options:  
('My answer is yes.', 'My answer is no.', 'My answer is maybe.', 'I think yes.', 'I think no.', 'I think maybe.')
